| P1A-004 | Defined names, merged ranges, sheet views | Done | Model: `CellRange`, `SheetView` (frozen panes), `Sheet.merges`/`view`, `DefinedName` (parsed Expr, workbook/sheet scope). Import parses mergeCells, sheetView pane, and workbook definedNames (localSheetId resolved). 19 tests (model 10 + import 9) |
| P1A-005 | Proper part discovery via content-types + all rels (not conventional paths) | Done | Import reads `[Content_Types].xml` overrides (`package.content_type_overrides()`) and walks relationships (`package.relationships_of()`) rather than guessing at conventional paths, which is what lets it open packages whose parts are not where the convention puts them. Content types are also carried onto retained rels so they survive a write. **Corrected from "Not started" during CI-003.** |
| P1A-006 | Retention mode + retained-source / opaque parts | Done | `Workbook::retained_parts` and `retained_rels` carry the parts this engine does not model — verbatim bytes plus their relationships and content types — through import, the model snapshot, and the writer, so opening and saving a file does not throw away the half of it OpenCalc has no opinion about. COL-11 established by test that retention survives a model snapshot, correcting a design document that had claimed otherwise. **Corrected from "Not started" during CI-003.** |
| IO-001 | Delimited text (CSV / TSV / PSV) reader + writer | Done | `casual-calc-io`: RFC 4180-style `read_delimited`/`write_delimited` (quoting, CRLF, field typing number/bool/text); `parse → write → parse` fixed point + quoting/delimiter tests (5). SDK `from_workbook`; WASM `session_open_delimited`/`session_save_delimited`; editor **Open** routes `.csv/.tsv/.psv` by extension and a **Download-as menu** exports xlsx/csv/tsv/psv (numbers use General formatting — no binary-float tails). Verified in-browser (quoted comma field preserved on import; clean `43.48` on export). ODS + format registry pending |
| UX-P07 | Real font picker (searchable, in-face preview) | Done | The font box only ever offered "Calibri" and the size box only "11": both were native `<input list=datalist>` comboboxes, and Chrome filters a datalist by the input's own text, so the better the box tracked the selection the more broken its dropdown looked. Replaced with a custom `.popmenu` combobox (full list from the caret, type-to-filter, check-marked current value, each family previewed in its own face, arbitrary typed values still accepted). The family list is engine-sourced — `PICKER_FAMILIES` in the substitution table, exposed as `font_families()` — so it can only offer families this build renders faithfully, and a metric/generic substitute says so in its tooltip. Four defects in the new code caught by an audit of it and fixed here (Enter on the "Default" row applied the literal name; Escape left the typed text and the following blur committed it; outside-click left `aria-expanded` stale; clicking a control inside a collapsed toolbar flyout dismissed the flyout). |
| UX-F01 | Surface-agnostic formula-editor controller (closes UX-E02) | Done | The formula bar was a plain text box: every piece of formula intelligence — autocomplete, click/drag reference picking, the rejected-formula outline, Escape-to-revert — was hard-wired to the in-cell editor, so the same typing that autocompleted in a cell did nothing up there. Introduced one **edit session** keyed to `editSurface` (whichever `<input>` holds the edit) with `beginEdit`/`endEdit`/`cancelEdit`/`mirrorEdit`; `currentFnToken`, `showAutocomplete`, `acceptAutocomplete`, `refAcceptable`, `insertRef`, the canvas mousedown commit path and `commit()`'s error affordance all read it instead of `inline`. Text mirrors between the two surfaces as you type, and focusing the bar mid-edit hands that same edit over rather than starting a second one. `.ac-menu` became `position: fixed` so one menu can anchor under either surface. Verified in-browser: autocomplete in the bar (SUM/SUMIF/…), single-click and drag reference picking from the bar (`=SUM(C5:E9`), Escape restoring the cell's text, Enter committing and advancing, Tab moving right, an invalid formula outlining the bar and keeping focus, click-away committing exactly once, and in-cell editing unchanged. Unlocks UX-F02/03/06/07. |
| UX-VIEW-HDR | View ▸ Headers — hide the row/column headers | Done | User request. Modelled on OOXML's own `showRowColHeaders`, mirroring what M2-2 did for gridlines, so the choice is per sheet, undoable and survives a save: `SheetView.hide_headers`, import/export of the attribute (a normal sheet writes nothing), `session_headers_hidden`/`session_set_headers_hidden`, and a checkmarked View-menu item. The editor's `HW`/`HH` header strips become 0 when hidden, so the grid simply runs to the corner and every hit-test, freeze origin and clip that measures the body as "past HW/HH" follows automatically. Round-trip test alongside the gridlines one; verified in-browser that a top-left click then selects A1 and the in-cell editor lands at 0,0. |
| REVIEW-FIXES | Regressions found by the adversarial review of this session's diff | Done | 18 findings, 11 refuted by the verifiers, 7 real and fixed: (1) `autofitRow` still used the old `cellLineH + 6` while `measure()` moved to `cellPx + 5`, so double-click autofit inflated an ordinary row 25% **and** pinned it there permanently (autofit persists the height, which `session_row_pinned` then reads as user-set); (2) `positionInline` built a straddling merge's box as one rect across the freeze line, which goes negative once the far half scrolls out, collapsing the editor to zero size; (3) the same function wrote raw grid coordinates with no lower bound, so the editor floated over the headers and frozen panes; (4)+(5) the text pass scanned the whole sheet's merge list per painted cell and swept every merge over the whole window each frame, making `draw()` O(visible cells × merges) on the unthrottled wheel path — both replaced by a per-frame `mergesByRow` index; (6) multi-area `sqref` cloned the value list per area with no cap; (7) `parse_theme` ignored the crate's element/depth bounds. |
| UX-F03a | Range finder — coloured outlines for a formula's references (with UX-F07) | Done | New `casual-calc-formula::reference_spans()` returns each reference in formula text with its **character span** and the block it covers; it lives next to the parser because the answers are the parser's (`SUM(` is a call while a bare `SUM` is a reference, and neither counts inside a string literal) and a host-side regex would drift. Exposed as `formula_ref_spans()`; the editor re-scans on every keystroke and on reference insertion, and `draw()` strokes one colour per reference from a six-colour palette, clipped per pane like the other overlays. `beginEdit` runs it too, so F2 on a stored formula highlights its inputs immediately (UX-F07). 6 unit tests including the function-call false positive, doubled quotes inside literals, `$` anchors, reversed ranges and quoted sheet qualifiers. Caught in review: `endEdit` cleared the spans without repainting, so outlines lingered after Escape. Tinting the reference tokens *inside the text* is the remaining half of UX-F03 — it needs an overlay behind the input, since an `<input>` cannot colour a substring. |
| BUG-FREEZEPANE | Overlays painted over — and slid across — frozen panes | Done | Four causes. (1) `spanX`/`spanY` clamped a range to the header edge, not to its pane: with fractional scroll the first body line is drawn a sliver *behind* the freeze line, so a body selection tinted a strip inside the frozen band whose width tracked the scroll offset (the sliding overlay), and the same code could not express "scrolled off" for a body range so the selection vanished instead. (2) The selection outline, marching ants and fill preview were never clipped per pane. (3) A merge straddling a freeze line was one rect with a pinned left edge and a scrolling right edge — once the body half scrolled out the width went negative (−236 px, measured) and canvas drew it flipped back over the frozen pane; it is now one slice per pane. (4) Merges positioned from engine offsets while everything else used the drawn grid, so auto-grown rows made them drift on every scroll step. Also: the in-cell editor (a DOM overlay) was positioned once and left behind by scrolling, and sized to the anchor rather than the merged block; and the merge toggle only unmerged on an exact-match selection. Verified in-browser against a straddling merge, a body-only merge, frozen-rows-only and no-freeze. |
| BUG-XLSXGEOM | Column widths and row heights not synced with uploaded files | Done | `<col>` parsing compared OOXML booleans against the literal `"1"`, but `xsd:boolean` also spells true as `"true"` — which LibreOffice, Apache POI and ExcelJS all write — so `customWidth` read false, the width was filed as the sheet default, and **every column width in such a file was discarded**; the same comparison dropped hidden/collapsed on both axes and `wrapText`. A `read_bool_attr()` accepting both already existed and simply was not used at these sites. Further: `customWidth` no longer gates whether a width is honored (it records autofit-vs-manual, not authority); only a sheet-wide span becomes the default, and it now beats an earlier `<sheetFormatPr>`. `<b val="0"/>`/`<i>`/`<strike>` no longer read as ON, and `<u val="none">` means no underline. Editor side: auto row-height no longer overrides a height the workbook set (`session_row_pinned`), no longer regrows hidden rows into visibility, and its tall-font metric is the font box plus Excel's leading — at 11 pt exactly the default row height, instead of inflating every styled row 25%. Verified by opening a LibreOffice-style file. |
| BUG-OVERFLOW | Text overflow wrong in frozen panes; numbers spilled | Done | The spill scan was bounded by `state.firstCol` — the first *scrolling* column — so left spill was dead inside the frozen band and a frozen cell laid its text out across the freeze line, relying on a downstream clip. Now bounded by the cell's own pane on both sides, so text is cut at the real boundary. Numbers spilled like text; a number clipped mid-digits still reads as a real, wrong value, so a number too wide for its column now renders `#######` and never spills (`session_cells` gained an `"n"` flag — the renderer previously could not tell a number from a right-aligned string). |
| BUG-SHAREDF | Shared formulas imported as bare constants | Done | Excel's fill-down writes the expression once on the group's master and leaves each follower as `<f t="shared" si="N"/>`; `si` was never read, so followers produced an empty formula, failed to parse, and were recorded Degraded — a filled column imported as one formula plus a stack of cached constants. Followers are rebuilt from their master and shifted by the row/column delta through `shift_references`, the same AST shifter fill and copy use, so `$` anchors hold. Test matrix covers the anchoring; verified end-to-end on a file whose followers cache `0` (recalculates to the right values). Also here: a multi-area `sqref` kept only its first area for both conditional formats and validations. |
| BUG-THEMECOL | Theme and indexed colors dropped on import | Done | Colors were read from the `rgb` attribute alone, but Excel's built-in cell styles state them as a `theme="N"` slot with an optional `tint`, or as a legacy `indexed="N"` entry — so a themed workbook imported unstyled. New `theme` module parses `xl/theme/theme1.xml` into the twelve-slot palette, applies tint by scaling luminance in HSL, and carries the indexed table; threaded through font/fill/border/dxf colors and the sheet tab color, with the stock Office scheme as the fallback. The slot order is *not* `<a:clrScheme>` order — the dk/lt pairs are swapped — which has its own test since getting it backwards turns black text white. Verified in-browser: accent fill + white bold text, a tinted fill, accent font color and an indexed yellow all render. |
| BUG-COMMENTBIND | Comments bound to the wrong sheet | Done | The importer guessed `xl/comments{sheet index + 1}.xml`, which only agrees with packages this writer produced; everyone else numbers comment parts by which sheets *have* comments, so in a workbook where only sheet 2 carries notes they landed on sheet 1. New `SpreadsheetPackage::related_part()` resolves a part's `.rels` and returns the target of a relationship type. The positional guess was removed rather than kept as a fallback — a comments part is unreachable except through a relationship, and the fallback is what produced the misbinding. |
| BUG-COLORAPPLY | Color popover Apply button overflowed, collapsing the hex field | Done | The base `.popmenu button { width:100% }` leaked onto `.cm-apply` (never overridden), so Apply took the full row and squeezed the hex input to nothing. Gave `.cm-apply` an explicit `width:auto`. |
| BUG-MERGESEL | Selecting a merged cell showed a spurious interior gridline | Done | The range border stroked a box around the raw selection = the merge's anchor cell (a sub-region), drawing an interior line inside the merge. Skip that stroke for single-cell selections; the focus-cell border already spans the whole merge. Verified in-browser. |
| M8-1 | Enhanced color popover (standard grid + custom hex + recent) | Done | Replaced the 6-swatch font/fill menus with a shared `buildColorMenu`: an Automatic/No-fill row, a 10×5 standard palette (grays + hues at 5 lightness levels), a live Recent row (last 10 picks, shared across both), and a custom `#RRGGBB` entry so any color the engine stores is reachable. Custom-toggled so the hex field doesn't close the menu; rebuilt on open to refresh Recent. Verified in-browser (custom hex e5484d → red text; Recent row populated). Theme swatches await the M10-1 token layer. |
| BUG-HIDE | Hide didn't collapse a row/column (only blanked content) | Done | `measure()` advanced positions with `geo.colW[i] || COL_W` / `rowH[i] || ROW_H`; a hidden line's legitimate `0` width is falsy, so it fell back to the default width — the line stayed full-size but empty instead of collapsing. Changed to `?? COL_W` / `?? ROW_H` so only missing values fall back. Verified in-browser: hiding column C now makes B and D adjacent. |
| M9-2 | Hidden-region markers + double-click-to-unhide | Done | Draw a small accent double-bar in the column/row header at each hidden gap (a run of zero-width lines), and double-clicking a marker unhides that band via `session_unhide_rows/cols`. Verified in-browser (hide C → marker between B and D → double-click → C returns). Hover arrows are a nice-to-have follow-up; Unhide-All is already in the Hide submenu. |
| M11-1a | Fix Replace All + add one-at-a-time Replace | Done | Bug: `session_find` searched `display_text` (numbers/formula results) but `session_replace_all` only rewrote pure-text cells, so Find matched cells Replace silently skipped. Now Find and both Replace paths operate on the cell's **editable input** (`cell_input_text` — `=formula` or value-as-typed, Excel's "Formulas" look-in) and re-parse via `build_set_op`, so text/numbers/formulas all replace and a match is always rewritable. Added `session_replace_at` (single cell) + a **Replace** button beside Replace all (replaces the current match, advances). Verified in-browser (Replace All: Widget→Sprocket; Replace: Gadget→Cog). Regex/match-entire-cell/all-sheets scope remain (M11-1). |
| M9-1 | Real row & column autofit honoring each cell's font/size/wrap | Done | `autofitColumn` measures with each cell's real font, and `autofitRow` honours font size, wrap and explicit newlines. **Rotation is now honoured too.** `autofitRow` carried its own copy of the row-measuring arithmetic, under a comment saying to match the shared `measureRowHeight` exactly — and the copy had no rotation case, so autofitting a row of rotated headings sized it as though the text were flat and clipped every one; since autofit *persists* the height, and a persisted height pins the row against further auto-growth, it did not correct itself. It calls the shared function now. That function gained the case the copy had and it lacked — explicit newlines without wrap — so the fix runs both ways and auto-growth is right for hard breaks too. `autofitColumn` projects rotated text onto the horizontal axis rather than sizing to the flat width, which had been making a rotated column several times wider than its contents; stacked text (`rot=255`) sizes to its widest single glyph. **Gated in-browser**: `session_row_height`/`session_col_width` were added — the height was written into the document with no way to read it back, so the only thing assertable about autofit was that it did not crash — and the test autofits a long heading flat, rotates it 90°, autofits again, and asserts the row more than doubles. Confirmed by reverting the fix: it reports `rotated 20 should exceed flat 20`, which is the clipping bug exactly. **Merged cells are excluded from autofit**, which is what Excel does and why. A title merged across A1:D1 is as wide as the whole table, so charging it to column A made column A as wide as the title — 390px for a column holding `7`. A merge spanning more than one column cannot size any one of them, and the same holds for rows and heights. Gated in-browser and confirmed by mutation: counting merged cells again reproduces the 390px column. M9-1 complete. |
| M8-2 | Full border palette (placements + line-style + color) | Done | Backend `session_set_border` now takes `kind` + `style` + `color`: 10 placements (all/inner/outer/horizontal/vertical/top/bottom/left/right/none) via a pure `border_edges` helper, additive (only sets the named edges), with per-edge OOXML line style (thin/medium/thick/dashed/dotted/double) and RRGGBB or auto color. Frontend: `buildBorderMenu` renders a 10-icon placement grid (SVG cell sketches), a line-style select, and a color-swatch row into a custom-toggled `#border-menu` (style/color set state without closing). Verified in-browser (blue All-borders on B2:C4). Excel combo presets (top+thick-bottom, etc.) left as niche extras. |
| M6-1 | Single-source function catalog (eval dispatch ↔ host autocomplete) | Done | Moved the `(name, signature)` catalog from a hand-maintained copy in `casual-calc-wasm` into `casual-calc-eval::FUNCTIONS`, next to the `call_function` dispatch. WASM `function_catalog` now reads it. Two drift tests: every catalog entry must have a dispatch arm (`NAME()` must not be #NAME?) and the list must stay sorted/unique. |
| M6-2 | High-frequency functions (29 added) | Done | Verified against the code during a reconciliation, not from memory: `TEXT` **is** implemented — dispatched in `functions.rs` and delegating to `casual_calc_layout::format_number`, which is the whole reason `casual-calc-eval` carries that downward edge. The row said it was the one thing outstanding; it had already shipped. Nothing remains from the M6-2 list. |
| M4-1 | Editable Name Box (parse A1/range, jump+select+scroll, live NR×NC) | Done | Already implemented in an earlier pass and verified now: the `#cell-ref` box parses a cell (`B12`) or range (`A1:C5`) and jumps with `ensureVisible`; shows `3R x 2C` while drag-selecting; jumps to a defined name via `session_name_target`; and defines a new name from the selection (Excel Name Box behavior). Ctrl+G/F5 focus it, Esc cancels. This also satisfies M4-2 (Go-To) and the M5-2 "Name Box accepts defined names" bullet. Verified in-browser (typed N18 → jumped). |
| M4-3 | Keyboard nav: Shift+Space / Ctrl+Space row/column selection | Done | `selectRowsSpan`/`selectColsSpan`: **Shift+Space** promotes the selection to its full rows, **Ctrl+Space** to its full columns, **Ctrl+Shift+Space** to all. Plain Space starts an inline edit whose first character is the space — recorded here as though it were an unfinished corner, and it is **what Excel does**: Space is the first character of the value being typed, and the row/column chords are its modified forms. Now gated by a browser test that presses both and reads the result, so the behaviour is a decision on the record rather than a line in a document nobody can check. Ctrl+Space is intercepted by macOS at the OS level for input-source switching, which is the OS's to own and not ours. |
| M3-5 | Visible-cells-only copy (skip filtered/hidden rows) | Done | Copy/cut now skips rows in `hidden_rows` and columns in `hidden_cols` (the filter routes through `session_hide_rows`, so both manual hide and column filters are covered) and compresses the survivors to contiguous offsets. `ClipCell` gained `sr`/`sc` (true source) so cut-clearing and per-cell formula ref-shift stay correct (shift is now per-cell `at - source`, identical to the old uniform delta when nothing is hidden). `session_copy_tsv`/`session_copy_html` skip hidden rows/cols too. Pure `clip_capture` extracted + unit-tested; verified in-browser (hid row 3, copied A2:D4, paste showed Widget+Gizmo contiguous, Gadget skipped). |
| M3-2 | Marching-ants source outline for copy/cut (pending-move affordance) | Done | The cut engine already worked (one-shot cut clears source on paste); this adds the visual. `clipToOS` starts an animated dashed outline (rAF loop advancing `lineDashOffset` ~every 80ms) around the copied/cut range, drawn via the existing `spanX/spanY` clip helpers so it respects frozen panes. Cleared on Esc, on the consuming cut-paste (`!session_clip_has()`), and on new/open. Verified in-browser (dashed box on B2:D4, gone on Esc). True Cut is already Ctrl+X-wired; further paste-move ref semantics unchanged. |
| M3-1 | Internal rich clipboard (value + formula + style, relative-ref shift) + HTML/TSV OS payload | Done | Internal clipboard was already in place: `session_clip_copy` captures value+style+formula AST relative to origin; `session_clip_paste_mode` reproduces them, shifting refs by the paste delta (absolute anchors held) with all/values/formats modes; cut clears the source in the same undo batch, one-shot. This increment adds the **HTML OS payload**: `session_copy_html` emits a styled `<table>` (bold/italic/underline/strike/color/fill/align → inline CSS, HTML-escaped) and `clipToOS` writes it as a `ClipboardItem` (`text/html` + `text/plain` TSV) so external apps (Excel/Sheets/mail/docs) receive formatting; text-only fallback when ClipboardItem is unavailable. 3 unit tests for the CSS/escape helpers; copy verified in-browser. Cross-app HTML paste-in is now tracked explicitly as UX-CLIP-01; its absence does not reopen this copy-out/internal-clipboard row. Marching ants shipped separately in M3-2. |
| UX-PANEL | Tool side panel + trimmed context menu (data validation / conditional formatting / notes) | Done | Grounded in competitive research (Sheets/Excel + Univer/OnlyOffice/Luckysheet): right-click menus stay ~12–18 "fast verb" items with sparse submenus, and heavy iterative editors go to a docked right panel (~300–360px). Built a single tool-switched `#side-panel` (opened from three new toolbar buttons; grid re-fits via `resize()`; live "Apply to range" readout; Esc/✕ close) hosting Data validation, Conditional formatting, and Notes — ported off the old floating ctx-menus. Trimmed `cellMenu` to Cut/Copy/Paste + submenus (Paste special / Insert / Delete / Hide / Clear / Sort). Verified in-browser. |
| UX-V02 | Per-column autofilter, moved into the engine | Done | The filter was **editor state**: one column at a time, checklist only, and `clearFilter()` unhid rows by calling `session_unhide_rows` — so it released rows the user had hidden by hand and nothing about it reached the saved file. Now a model object. `Sheet::auto_filter` holds a header `CellRange` plus a `FilterRule` per column *offset* (OOXML's `colId` basis, so the mapping round-trips): `Values` for a checklist, `Custom` for one or two comparisons joined AND/OR. Contains / begins-with / ends-with are **not** separate operators — they are `equal` plus `*`/`?` wildcards, which is how Excel stores them, so the host encodes and the writer emits exactly that. Wildcard matching is iterative with one backtrack point, so a pathological pattern stays O(text × pattern) instead of going exponential. **The rows a filter hides live in `Sheet::filter_hidden`, apart from `hidden_rows`** — that split is the fix for the release-the-wrong-rows bug, and every reader now goes through `Sheet::is_row_hidden` (geometry, the wasm row-size/copy paths, the writer's `hidden="1"`). Evaluation sits in the wasm layer, not the model, because a checklist matches **display text** and formatting only exists there; it runs on filter change and on load, not per keystroke, which is both Excel's behaviour and what keeps an edit from costing a range scan. Filter state rides on `SetSheetMetadata`, so rules and hidden rows undo together, and `structural.rs` shifts both through row insert/delete (dropping the filter when its whole range is deleted). Import/export do `<autoFilter>` end to end, including `<filters blank="1">` for the (Blanks) entry. UI: a dropdown button per header cell (accented where a rule is active), a searchable checklist that is **chained** — it offers only the values the other columns' rules leave — and a two-comparison condition dialog. Also fixed in passing: the writer emitted `dataValidations` **before** `conditionalFormatting`, the reverse of the CT_Worksheet sequence. Verified in-browser: buttons draw, two columns AND together, the chained checklist narrows, `>15` compares numerically, clearing the filter leaves a hand-hidden row hidden, and undo restores rules + hidden rows in one step. 12 new tests (6 model, 4 transaction, 3 export round-trip incl. schema order). |
| UX-V04 | Outline / grouping UX + engine surface | Done | The model had carried outline levels, collapsed flags and `<outlinePr>` since import/export landed, but there was no way to make or toggle a group — the row read "model support, zero UX". Added `Sheet::outline_band`, which resolves what a summary line controls: the contiguous run nesting deeper than it, *above* when `summaryBelow` (the OOXML default) and below otherwise, never including the summary itself. wasm gains `session_group`/`session_ungroup` (± a level, capped at OOXML's 7), `session_toggle_outline`, `session_show_outline_level` and `session_outline`; the last returns just the maximum depth when asked for zero lines, so a sheet with no outline costs one cheap call a frame. Editor draws an **outline gutter** beside each header strip, sized from the deepest level and absent otherwise; `HW`/`HH` absorb its width so every existing geometry consumer follows it without knowing it exists. Data ▸ Group covers group/ungroup, expand/collapse all and show-level. **Two things worth recording.** A collapse hides via `hidden_rows` (what OOXML writes), so the hidden-band handle from UX-P11 also appears over a collapsed group — clicking it revealed the rows while leaving the group flagged collapsed, so the toggle showed `+` over plainly visible rows. `hidden_edit` now clears any collapse flag whose band is no longer fully hidden. And `SetSheetMetadata` grew to twelve collections; unboxed it padded **every** variant of `Operation` — including the `SetCell` a bulk edit pushes thousands of — to 352 bytes, which clippy flagged. Extracted `SheetMetadata` with `capture`/`install` and boxed it, taking the enum back under 128 bytes (asserted by a test) and making the next positional field a one-place change. Verified in-browser: group, rail and toggle at the summary, collapse, and unhide-by-handle leaving the toggle correctly expanded. 6 new model tests (band resolution incl. nesting and both summary placements) + 2 transaction tests. |
| UX-FM4 | Full OOXML alignment set — model, round-trip and render | Done | Started as "add the missing menu items" and turned out to be a **silent fidelity bug**: `HAlign::from_ooxml` folded `centerContinuous` into `Center`, and `VAlign::from_ooxml` folded `distributed` into `Middle` and `justify` into `Bottom`. Opening and saving a file therefore rewrote its alignment, with nothing reported. Both enums now carry the complete token set (`fill`, `justify`, `centerContinuous`, `distributed`; vertical `justify`, `distributed`) and a round-trip test pins all twelve. Extending the enums made the compiler point at every consumer, which is how the HTML-clipboard CSS mapping got fixed too (CSS has no `fill`/`centerContinuous`, so those fall back to the edge the text starts from via a new `HAlign::base_edge`). Render: Fill repeats the text across the cell; Center-across centres over the run of cells carrying the mode; Justify/Distributed stretch inter-word gaps per wrapped line, Distributed including the last; vertical Justify/Distributed spread the lines down the cell. **A bug caught in review of my own work**: Center-across first spanned every *empty* neighbour, which flung a lone label into the middle of the viewport — OOXML marks the group by putting `centerContinuous` on each cell, so the span follows that, and `session_cells` now emits such cells even when otherwise bare so the host can see the run. Also `session_set_valign` carried a hand-rolled token match that silently dropped the new values; it defers to `VAlign::from_ooxml` now. Verified in-browser: fill, centre-across over A6:D6 unmerged, and justified wrapped text flush to both margins. Vertical justify/distributed are implemented but not yet visually verified. |
| UX-FM6 | Colour picker: workbook theme row, tint ladder, tolerant colour input | Done | The picker offered a fixed palette and a hex field that took only 6-digit hex, and had no theme colours. It now shows the workbook's own theme row plus Excel's tint ladder, and accepts `#abc`, `rgb(...)` and `hsl(...)`. **Theme linkage round-trips too**, which this row recorded as outstanding: a theme swatch carries its slot and tint through `pick` into `setFill`/`setFontColor` — passed by reference as the picker's `onPick`, which is why searching for their call sites finds none — and on into `session_set_fill`. The model keeps it as `ThemeTint`, the exporter writes `theme="N"`, the importer reads it back. Every piece existed; nothing asserted the path end to end, so it went on being read as unfinished. Now gated by a round-trip test and verified by mutation: dropping the theme on export fails it. |
| UX-FM7 | Named cell styles — model, round-trip and gallery | Done | The row said "absent", but the gap was bigger than a missing menu: import read only `cellXfs`, so `cellStyleXfs` and `cellStyles` were never parsed, and the writer hardcoded a single anonymous `cellStyleXf` with `xfId="0"` on every cell. Visual formatting survived (it is resolved into `cellXfs`), but the *association* did not — a Heading 1 cell came back as bold-and-blue, invisible to Excel's gallery and unreachable by restyling the name. Added `Workbook::cell_styles` (`NamedCellStyle`: name, `builtinId`, formatting) and `Style::style_ref` for the `xf/@xfId` link — and taught `Style::is_default` about it, the trap that has silently dropped styles twice before. Import parses both elements, shares one `Xf`→`Style` conversion with `cellXfs` so they cannot resolve differently, and drops `xfId` 0 (Normal says nothing, and keeping it would make every plain cell "styled" and stop deduplicating). Export writes real tables through a new shared `write_xf`, and **emits Normal in cellStyleXfs slot 0** regardless of where it sat in the source, remapping every link — unlinked cells resolve `xfId="0"` to that slot, so getting it wrong would silently restyle them. `builtinId` is preserved because Excel keys its gallery off the id, not the localized name. UI: Format ▸ Cell styles… previewing each entry in its own look, workbook definitions taking precedence over the stock ten. Verified in-browser: gallery renders with per-style previews, applying Good restyles the cell, and reopening shows Good once (the workbook's own) rather than duplicated against the builtin. 2 round-trip tests. |
| UX-FM1b | Conditional formatting: ranked/statistical rules, priority, dxf font effects, Manage Rules | Done | Closes UX-FM1 (colour scales and data bars landed in 7482dbd). Added `Top10` (top/bottom N and N%), `AboveAverage` and `DuplicateValues`/unique. These need the *whole range*, not the cell, so the old `is_range_relative` split was renamed into two honest predicates: `needs_range_stats` (cannot be decided per cell) and `has_own_presentation` (paints itself instead of via a dxf) — the old name covered both and would have been wrong for the new kinds. `session_cells` now builds a `CfStats` per rule once per call: min, max, mean, a **presorted top-N cutoff** so the per-cell test is a comparison rather than a re-sort, and value counts built only when a duplicate rule asks for them (otherwise it would allocate a string per cell for nothing). Modelled `priority` and `stopIfTrue`, and evaluation now runs in priority order with a matching stop-if-true ending the search — previously first-in-document-order won regardless. Dxf gains font colour and bold, and a CF font colour outranks the cell's own since the rule is a statement about *that* value. Manage Rules dialog lists rules in evaluation order with reorder (which rewrites priorities to a dense 1..n so order never depends on tie-breaks), stop-if-true and delete. **A bug worth recording**: the new rules are childless, so they serialize as self-closing `<cfRule/>` and arrive as `Event::Empty` — the reader only handled `cfRule` in the `Start` dispatch, so they wrote correctly and imported as nothing. The construction is now a shared helper called from both. Added `ConditionalFormat::new` so the four new fields did not have to be spelled at every call site. Verified in-browser: all 16 kinds offered, ranked and duplicate rules apply, Manage Rules lists/reorders/deletes. 2 round-trip tests. |
| UX-G01 | Drawn vs engine geometry for auto-grown rows | Done | `measure()` grew a row for wrapped text, but scroll anchoring, the frozen-band origin, the scrollbar extent, `ensureVisible` and the row-resize origin all read the engine's un-grown offsets — so a wrapped row above the viewport made every one of them wrong by the accumulated growth: a hitch at row boundaries while wheel-scrolling, a short scrollbar, under-scrolling that let the selection creep off screen, and a resize drag that started offset. The two candidate fixes were "push auto-height into the engine" or "keep a prefix sum in `measure()`"; neither is quite right on its own, because **text measurement is browser-side** — the engine cannot compute the height, but the host measuring only what it can see is exactly the bug. So: the engine reports the *candidates* (`session_autofit_candidates` — every cell that could grow a row, excluding rows the workbook sized itself since an explicit height beats auto-fit, plus the sheet default so the host needs no call per row), and the host measures them once into a sparse row→growth map with prefix sums. `rowOffsetPx`/`rowAtPx` fold the growth in, and all nine call sites now go through them; the inverse converges by re-asking the engine after subtracting the growth above the current guess, which terminates because growth is monotonic. Extracted `neededRowHeight` so the per-frame measure and the growth map cannot disagree about a height — if they ever did, the drawn rows and the scroll offsets would disagree too. Invalidated on edit, zoom, column resize, sheet switch and load; column widths looked up once per distinct column rather than per cell. The candidate list is capped at 20k and reports truncation in the status bar rather than silently handing back a partial map. Verified in-browser: with a tall wrapped row on the sheet, Go-To A200 lands with the row on screen and the grown row renders without drift. |
| UX-P13b | Sheet protection (round-trip) + tab overflow/all-sheets menu | Done | Closing the rest of the sheet-tabs row, and the protection half was **another silent loss**: `<sheetProtection>` was neither parsed nor written, so a protected sheet came back unprotected — and the `<protection>` element inside an xf went the same way, unlocking every cell the author had locked. Both round-trip now, with a test that pins the password hash byte-for-byte. The sheet element is kept **attribute-for-attribute** rather than modelled as typed flags: it mixes a dozen permissions with a hash, salt, algorithm and spin count, and getting any of those wrong on the way out is worse than not reading them — a regenerated hash locks the author out of their own sheet, a dropped flag grants a permission they withheld. `Style::is_default` was taught about the two new fields, the trap that has now sprung four times. **The `Event::Empty` trap again**: `<sheetProtection>` is self-closing in every real file, so handling it only in the `Start` dispatch meant it wrote correctly and imported as nothing — the round-trip test caught it, exactly as it did for `cfRule`. UI: protected tabs marked, all-sheets menu listing hidden sheets (jumping to one reveals it rather than stranding the view), active tab scrolled into view on overflow. **Protection is preserved, not enforced** — recorded on the fidelity ledger rather than implied. |
| UX-V01a | Data validation: all OOXML kinds, modelled and enforced | Done | The importer accepted `type="list"` and nothing else, so number, date, time, text-length and custom rules were **dropped on save** — the nastiest shape of that bug, because the cell still looks constrained in Excel until someone reopens the file and finds it is not. `DataValidation` now carries kind, operator, both operands, `allowBlank` and the author's input/error wording; `DvKind` and `DvOperator` cover the full token sets. Enforcement is `DataValidation::accepts` **in the model**, so the rule and the check cannot drift apart, with two deliberate abstentions: a `Custom` rule returns `None` ("cannot judge here") rather than blocking input on a formula this layer does not evaluate, and an unparseable operand — typically a range reference — rejects nothing rather than making the cell uneditable. The author's own error text always beats the generated phrasing. Two bugs found while building it: `allowBlank`'s schema default is **false**, not true as I first modelled it (the round-trip test caught the flip); and `session_validation_at` returned an empty array for non-list rules, which JS reads as truthy, so a whole-number cell grew a dropdown chevron opening onto nothing. Verified in-browser: 50 into a 1..10 whole-number cell is refused with the author's message and the editor held open; 7 into an unvalidated cell commits normally. 6 model tests + 2 round-trip tests. |
| UX-F08a | Trace precedents / dependents | Done | The eval crate already walked a formula's precedents to build its incremental dirty set, so this **exposes that walk** rather than writing a second one — which matters more than it sounds: a trace built from its own parser could disagree with what recalculation actually follows, and the arrow would then be lying at exactly the moment someone is using it to find a bug. `precedents_of` returns blocks, so a range precedent is one arrow rather than one per cell. `dependents_of` scans every formula instead of maintaining a reverse index, a deliberate trade: tracing is an occasional, explicit action, and a reverse map would have to be kept correct on every edit to serve something asked once in a while. The two callbacks could not share a mutable accumulator, so cells and ranges collect separately and merge. UI draws a box per block plus an arrow whose **direction carries the meaning** — into the cell for precedents, out of it for dependents — colour-coded, with cross-sheet hits counted in the status line rather than dropped, since they have nowhere on screen to point. Verified in-browser both ways on `D5 = SUM(D2:D4)`. |
| UX-P15a | Import/export UX + delimited field-typing fidelity | Done | Encoding is now settled before parsing (UTF-16 LE/BE by BOM, UTF-8 BOM stripped, BOM-less UTF-16 sniffed from its zero bytes) and failures are reported in terms of the file. The substantive part was field typing: `007` imported as `7` and ISO dates were never dates, which made `casual-calc-io`'s own documented `parse → write → parse` fixed point false — a zip code did not survive one round trip. Padding zeros now keep the field as text and ISO 8601 dates/times become serials carrying a matching number format, with export rendering them back through that format instead of writing `45356`. Locale-ambiguous `3/5/2024` is deliberately left as text: nothing in the file distinguishes March from May, and guessing would make the same bytes import differently on two machines, which the determinism contract forbids. `parse_iso_datetime`/`is_date_format` are published from `casual-calc-io` and reused by the session so typing a date and importing one cannot drift; the formula bar shows a date cell as its date, and a user-chosen `dd/mm/yyyy` survives a retype. Two live bugs fixed alongside: opening hung whenever the tab was backgrounded (the paint-yield awaited `requestAnimationFrame`, which does not fire off-screen), and `session_cell_format` emitted `"nf"` twice, producing duplicate-key JSON. 6 io tests + 1 session test; verified in-browser end to end including `=B3-B2` → 660 days. |
| UX-P12a | Threaded comments: model, OOXML round-trip, thread UI | Done | `CellComment` becomes a thread — opening remark, replies, per-entry author and timestamp, resolved flag — with one type covering both shapes, since a plain note is just a thread with no replies and no timestamp. That keeps the editor from having to ask which kind a cell holds, and lets a note become a thread on its first reply instead of migrating between representations. Writes the real 2018 parts (`xl/threadedComments/*`, `xl/persons/*`) and only when a thread needs them, so a bare note still produces the legacy part alone. Three things the schema forced: replies are **siblings** carrying `parentId`, not nested children; Excel writes a legacy note alongside every thread for pre-2018 readers, so import must *replace* the legacy record rather than keep both; and the GUIDs are derived from the thread's coordinates rather than randomly, because a random one would make two saves of the same workbook differ. Timestamps are supplied by the host so the engine stays a pure function of its inputs. UI: right-click reads Insert comment / Show comments by what is actually there, the panel's primary verb switches Save → Reply, the hover shows the whole conversation, and the author is asked once and kept. Also fixed: an unsigned note came back attributed to "OpenCalc" — an empty author slot is the schema's own way of writing anonymous, so it now stays unsigned. 5 tests (round-trip, resolved+authorless, determinism, no-threaded-parts-for-a-plain-note, XML shape vs the persons list); verified in-browser through post → reply → resolve. |
| UX-FM6a | Theme colour linkage: slot + tint survives a save | Done | A `Style` now keeps the resolved `RRGGBB` **and** a `ThemeTint { slot, tint_micro }` beside it when the colour came from the theme. Keeping both rather than replacing one with the other means nothing downstream of the model needs the theme to paint a cell, while the file still states a slot — so Excel moves the cell when the workbook is re-themed, which a bare `RRGGBB` never does. Tint is an integer count of millionths because `Style` is `Hash + Eq` (styles deduplicate by value) and a float is neither; a millionth is finer than 8-bit channels can express and re-reads to the same integer, so the round trip stays a fixed point. Colours are set through `set_font_color`/`set_fill_color`, which drop a stale link instead of letting the file claim one colour while showing another. Two things this exposed: the writer had to start shipping `xl/theme/theme1.xml`, because a `theme="4"` with no theme part resolves against the *reader's* default palette — a re-themed workbook would have reopened in stock Office colours, and our own importer would have re-read different RGB than we wrote; and `<a:clrScheme>` lists `dk1` before `lt1` while a `theme="N"` attribute indexes `lt1` first, so writing the scheme in slot order inverts black and white text. A test pins that pair. The picker passes the slot through (`-1` meaning "not from the theme"), so a swatch off the theme row stays linked and a hand-picked colour stays literal. Still resolved-only: border and conditional-format colours. 2 tests + full round trip; verified in-browser across base, tinted and standard swatches. |
| UX-A03a | Accessibility tree: DOM mirror of the visible grid | Done | The canvas now owns a DOM mirror of the on-screen cells (`#grid-a11y` via `aria-owns`), so assistive tech has real rows, column headers and row headers to move through instead of a live region and nothing else. Focus stays on the canvas and `aria-activedescendant` names the mirrored active cell — the pattern ARIA defines for a widget whose content is not focusable. Only the visible window is mirrored, since a million cells of DOM would defeat the point of a canvas; that stays invisible to the reader because each cell carries its absolute `aria-rowindex`/`aria-colindex`. The mirror is built from `geo.rowIdx`/`geo.colIdx` — the indices the renderer just drew — so a hidden or filtered row is missing from it for the same reason it is missing from the screen, rather than through a second notion of visibility that could drift. Two details decide whether it is usable rather than merely present: an empty cell needs an `aria-label`, because a cell with no accessible name is skipped by some readers and the columns then misalign as a row is read across; and `aria-rowcount` has to be the navigable extent (the +30 rows / +8 columns the scrollbars already offer) rather than the used range, or scrolling below the data announces "row 40 of 6". Rebuilds are gated on a signature including the cell payload, so an unchanged view does not churn the tree. Verified in-browser end to end, including 60 wheel events through the full draw path in 23.5 ms total — no cost on the scroll path. |
| FID-1904 | Date epoch (1904) + Excel's 1900 leap-year bug | Done | Found by auditing what the writer emits against CT_Workbook rather than by a tracker row. **Nothing anywhere handled `workbookPr/@date1904`.** A 1904-epoch workbook (legacy Mac Excel) was read as if it were 1900, so every date displayed 1462 days — over four years — off; and because the flag was not written either, saving left the serials untouched while their meaning shifted, corrupting every date in the file permanently. Modelled on `Workbook`, parsed from `workbook.xml`, written as the leading `<workbookPr>` child (by omission for the 1900 default), and threaded into rendering via `format_number_1904` — the offset applies only to date and time sections, since a plain number means the same under either epoch and shifting the raw value would corrupt it. `display_text` and the format-dialog preview both honour it. Writing the test then exposed a **second** bug: Excel's serial 1 is 1900-01-01, but ours rendered 1899-12-31 — the conversion was anchored past Excel's phantom 1900-02-29, so every date before March 1900 was a day short and serial 60 was unrepresentable. Both are now reproduced deliberately, because disagreeing here means disagreeing with the file's author about what a date is. 2 tests. |
| FC-00 | Feature correctness tracker (53) from a code scan | Done | The fidelity phase answered "does a workbook survive a round trip" and reached 95.9% structural with no destructive gaps. It cannot answer whether the app does the right thing *through the model* for the features it already claims, so 53 asks five independent questions per feature — reachable, model-backed, undoable, round-tripping, rendered — and generates its rows from the code rather than from recollection. The finding that matters: **fourteen mutations write straight to `workbook_mut()` and never build an `Operation`**, so undo does not merely fail for conditional formatting, comments, validation, named styles, sheet visibility and sheet protection — it reverses *the previous cell edit instead*, destroying work the user did not ask to lose. Also: hyperlinks, tables, print setup and charts round-trip correctly with **zero** UI to create or edit them, and 70 modelled fields are read by nothing outside the file layer, so rich text round-trips exactly and renders as uniform plain text. The tool caught its own instance of the same defect first: reading function bodies alone, it reported every toolbar styling command as un-undoable when they all delegate to `apply_style_range`, which builds an `EditOperation::Batch`. Following one level of indirection fixed it. |
| M1-4 | Split Clear into Contents / Formats / All; Delete key keeps formatting | Done | Clear Contents (keep style), Delete→clear-contents, and Clear All were already in place. Added the missing `session_clear_formats` (drops style via SetStyle{None}, keeps value+formula, recalc-skipped) + a "Clear formats" context-menu item between the two. Also closed the hide/unhide direct-mutation gap from M1-1 (now undoable). |
| M1-3 | Sheet rename rewrites cross-sheet formula refs + triggers recalc | Done | `casual-calc-formula::rename_sheet_references` walks the AST rewriting `Old!A1` → `New!A1` (case-insensitive, bare refs untouched). The transaction `RenameSheet` op applies it workbook-wide after the name swap; its existing inverse reverses the rewrite. sdk classifies RenameSheet as Full recalc. 6 tests (4 formula-rewrite + rename-rewrite-and-undo in transaction). Verified in-browser (rename keeps totals, no #REF). Delete already yields #REF via name resolution + full recalc; baking #REF into the AST on delete (to survive same-name recreation) is a deferred refinement given the name-based model. |
| M8-4 | Editable font-size combobox + grow/shrink buttons | Done | The font-size control was a fixed `<select>` (8–36 presets), so you couldn't type sizes like 13 or 22, and a cell with such a size showed blank. Replaced it with an editable `<input list=datalist>` combobox: type any size (clamped to Excel's 1–409 pt; blank/0 clears), keep the presets in the dropdown, Enter commits + returns focus to the grid, Esc reverts, focus selects-all. Added **A▲/A▼ grow/shrink buttons** that step the active cell's size along a standard ladder (8,9,10,11,12,14,16,18,20,24,28,36,48,72; ±2 beyond it). Live-verified: typed 22 (not a preset) → renders at 22pt with row auto-grow; grow from default 11 → 12 → 14, shrink → 12. Searchable in-face font menu is a minor remaining nice-to-have. |
| M4-5 | Persist per-sheet selection & scroll on sheet switch | Done | `switchSheet` slammed every sheet back to A1 (via `resetView`). Now a `sheetViews` map remembers each sheet's scroll + selection + selKind, saved on leave and restored on return. **Keyed by sheet name, not index** — the first attempt keyed by index and cleared the map on sheet-count change, which wiped the just-saved view during the very add that switched away (caught and fixed before commit); name-keying survives add/delete/reorder/undo with no index-shift bug (a rename just drops that one sheet's remembered view). Live-verified: Sheet1 at C40 → add Sheet2 (opens at A1) → back to Sheet1 restores C40 with the scrolled viewport. Persisting activeTab into the .xlsx is a later refinement. |
| M9-3 | Insert/delete row/column above-below-left-right variants + Ctrl +/- shortcuts | Done | The structural insert/delete ops already existed (and shift merges/hidden/freeze/refs, tested in the transaction crate). Added the missing directional menu variants — Insert now offers **Row above / Row below / Column left / Column right** (below = `r1+1`, right = `c1+1`) — and **Ctrl+Shift+"+" / Ctrl+"-"** shortcuts via `insertLines`/`deleteLines`, which pick the axis from the selection kind (whole-column selection → columns, whole-row → rows, else rows, per Excel). Live-verified: Ctrl+"-" deleted the Price column (Total formulas correctly became #REF!), Ctrl+Shift+"+" inserted a column when a column was selected, and all four Insert variants are present in the menu. Delete-referenced warning is a nice-to-have follow-up. |
| M6-2b | TEXT function (closes the M6-2 function set) | Done | The last deferred M6-2 function. `casual-calc-eval` gains a **downward** dependency on `casual-calc-layout` (layout sits below eval in the DAG; no cycle — layout → model only) to reuse the display's pure `format_number(f64,&str)->String`, so TEXT and on-screen cell display format identically and never drift. `eval_text` coerces the value (numbers formatted via the engine, bool→TRUE/FALSE, text unchanged) and the format code (as_text). Catalog entry added (single-source, so the editor autocomplete picks it up); catalog drift test now covers it. Unit-tested: `TEXT(1234.5,"#,##0.00")`→"1,234.50", `"0%"`→"25%", multi-section negative `$#,##0.00;[Red]($#,##0.00)` on -42 → "($42.00)". Fully value-based (no reference plumbing), so no correctness traps. |
| M2-2 | Show/hide gridlines flag + sheetView showGridLines round-trip | Done | Added `SheetView.hide_gridlines` (default false = shown, matching OOXML; skip-if-false serialize; folded into `is_default`). Import parses `<sheetView showGridLines="0">` → `hide_gridlines=true`; export emits `showGridLines="0"` only when hidden (a normal sheet writes nothing). Round-trip unit-tested (default → no attribute; hidden → attribute present → survives import→write→import; new `xml_of` test helper reads the written part). WASM `session_gridlines_hidden` getter + undoable `session_set_gridlines_hidden` (via SetSheetMetadata snapshot); editor gates the canvas gridline stroke on it (selection tint still paints) with a "Toggle gridlines" item in the freeze menu. **Live-verified: Toggle gridlines hides the canvas grid (selection tint + headers still render); toggling again restores them.** |
| M3-3 | Paste Special: Formulas-only + Transpose | Done | Extended `session_clip_paste_mode` beyond values/formats/all: **`formulas`** pastes each cell's value + reference-shifted formula while keeping the *target's* existing style (reads the target's StyleId, StyleId is Copy so the borrow closes before store_formula's mutable borrow), and **`transpose`** swaps the per-cell row/col offsets so the block rotates about its top-left origin (formula refs shift by the transposed per-cell delta). Editor Paste-special submenu gains "Formulas only" and "Transpose". Unlike M4-4, this is unit-tested end-to-end: a native test drives the real `session_*` thread-local functions (copy → paste-special → read back), asserting transpose rotates + ref-shifts (`=A1*10` → `=(B4*10)`) and formulas-only keeps the target's bold while pasting `=A1+1` → `=(D4+1)`. All CI gates re-run clean. |
| M4-4 | Header drag-select + Shift-extend + Ctrl/Cmd multi-select of rows/cols, with edge auto-scroll | Done | Column/row header mousedown now starts a `state.headerDrag` ("col"/"row"): plain click selects the whole column/row, Shift extends from the anchor (`selectColumn/selectRow(c, true)`), Ctrl/Cmd banks a range and starts a fresh whole-column/row (`addColumnRange`/`addRowRange`, mirroring `addRange`). Dragging across headers extends the band under the pointer; edge auto-scroll (`autoScrollTick`) keeps extending columns/rows during the scroll (not collapsing to a cell), and `edgeVelocity` restricts a col-drag to horizontal scroll (row-drag to vertical). `state.headerDrag` cleared on mouseup. **Live-verified: drag B→D header selected 3 columns ("1R x 3C", Sum 113.45), and Ctrl+click column F added a discontiguous band.** |
| M5-1 | Undoable defined-name ops (define/rename/delete) | Done | `session_define_name`/`session_delete_name` already existed (plus `session_names`/`session_name_target`, wired to the Name Box since M4-1) but mutated `workbook_mut()` directly and called `recalculate()` manually — not undoable, same bug class M1-1 fixed for sheet ops. Added `Operation::SetDefinedNames(Vec<DefinedName>)` to the transaction crate — a whole-table snapshot swap mirroring `SetSheetMetadata`, self-inverse; classified `Full` recalc in the sdk (a name change affects what any formula referencing it resolves to). Both WASM ops now build the new table and route through `session.edit`. 2 new transaction tests (self-inverse + `History` undo/redo). Dependency-graph resolution for names beyond blanket-full-recalc is a further-out refinement, not required to close this item. Gates re-run clean (fmt/clippy -D warnings/test/docs/wasm32); browser extension was disconnected this turn so live verification relies on the transaction-level tests, which exercise the exact op the WASM layer emits. |
| M8-3 | Number format engine multi-section depth & toolbar decimal controls | Done | Added multi-section format code resolution (Positive;Negative;Zero;Text) & section selection, quote/bracket-aware section splitting, and adjust_format_decimals in casual-calc-layout::numfmt (with 6 unit tests). `?` digit-alignment placeholders (e.g. the Accounting preset's zero-section `"-"??`) now pad with spaces instead of leaking the literal `?` character. Added session_adjust_decimals WASM binding (batch undoable). Added Comma, Increase Decimals (.0+), Decrease Decimals (.0-), and expanded preset dropdown (incl. Accounting) in editor.html/js. Verified via cargo test --workspace and live in-browser (decimal +/− with correct undo, Accounting negative → `$(42.50)`, Accounting zero → `$-` with no stray `?`). All CI gates (fmt/clippy -D warnings/test/docs/wasm32) re-run clean before push. |
| M1-1 | Route all sheet & structural mutations through commit_edit (undoable + dirties doc) | Done | Keystone. Transaction crate gains InsertSheet/RemoveSheet/RenameSheet/MoveSheet/SetTabColor invertible ops (8 new tests, self-inverse + History undo/redo); sdk `recalc_plan` classifies them (sheet add/remove/rename → Full recalc for name resolution, move/tab-color → Skip). WASM sheet ops (add/rename/delete/move/duplicate), freeze, tab-color, merge/unmerge, and resize-all moved off direct `workbook_mut()` onto `session.edit` (freeze/merge/resize-all reuse SetSheetMetadata via a metadata-snapshot helper). editor.js `doUndo`/`doRedo` now `renderTabs()` (re-clamps the active sheet). Verified in-browser: add→undo removes the sheet, redo restores it. Single/range/clear col-row width were already routed. Hide/unhide `hidden_edit` was still mutating `workbook_mut()` directly — routed through SetSheetMetadata alongside M1-4. Data-feature ops (validation/CF/comments/names) undoability is a follow-up. |
| UX-B01 | Four reported bugs from real use: table filter menu empty, Cmd+A too wide, no sub/superscript control, table not discoverable — plus filter buttons on empty columns | Done | All five were the same two mistakes. **(1) A table's filter had nowhere to live.** `Table` carried `auto_filter_ref: Option<String>` — the range only — and every filter operation read `sheet.auto_filter`, so a table's header button found no filter covering its column and returned an empty checklist. `Table::auto_filter: Option<AutoFilter>` now holds real rules; import and export share one `build_auto_filter`/`write_auto_filter` instead of two implementations that had already drifted (the table's read only the `ref` and dropped every `<filterColumn>` silently on load). Wasm resolves a `FilterSite` from the column rather than assuming the sheet, and `recompute_filter_hidden` unions across all filters so clearing one does not release rows another hides. New `session_filter_regions` reports `{hidden, regions}` — the hidden count is there because `session_filter_info` reports nothing when the sheet has no filter of its own, which made the status line say "filter cleared" on the edit that had just hidden two rows. **(2) Two commands took the used region where they meant the current one.** Ctrl+A and the toolbar filter both spanned A1 to the last used cell — "everything" on a loaded sheet, and filter buttons on columns blank in the header row. Both now use `session_block_bounds`, which Ctrl+T already used; Ctrl+A follows Excel's block → used region → whole sheet progression. Also added `session_toggle_vert_align` (sub/superscript had been modelled, imported, exported and drawn since FC-13 with no way to apply it) and Insert-menu entries for Table and Hyperlink. Verified in-browser: table filter lists values and hides rows, undo restores them in one step, Ctrl+A selects the block, superscript draws and undoes, and a filter applied next to a stray far-off cell covers only the table. All gates clean. |
| PIV-01 | Pivot tables: model, engine, import, undoable host surface | Done | A pivot is a **query** and its answer is **ordinary cells** — which is what Excel stores too, so a workbook we refresh opens in a reader that has never heard of pivot tables. `PivotTable` holds the query; `casual-calc-eval::pivot` answers it. Every record accumulates into **every (row-prefix, column-prefix) pair in one pass**, which is what subtotals and grand totals *are* — the grand total is the empty prefix on both axes — so they cost one traversal instead of one per level and cannot disagree with the rows above them. Six running numbers per accumulator give all eleven aggregates, so `Average` beside `Sum` is free. Layout is **tabular, not compact**: compact carries the field structure in an indent, which makes `A5` a region or a product depending on how far it is pushed in, and nothing downstream can tell. Refresh **refuses rather than overwrites** and is a command, never a side effect of an edit — both Excel's behaviour and for the same reasons. Import parses `pivotTableDefinition` + `pivotCacheDefinition` so an imported pivot is *live*, while the parts stay retained and untouched until it is refreshed; refreshing then drops them, because our tabular report and Excel's compact part would describe different things and a reader believes the part. **A latent bug surfaced on the way in: `<pivotCaches>` was being dropped entirely** — cache parts and their relationship retained with nothing in `workbook.xml` declaring them, which Excel reports as a file needing repair. It follows `<calcPr>` in CT_Workbook's sequence, not `<sheets>`, so it needed its own wrapper in its own place. 28 tests. See [docs/54-PIVOT-TABLES.md](54-PIVOT-TABLES.md). |
| PIV-03 | Pivot panel in the editor | Done | Excel's PivotTable Fields pane in one column: a source field list you drag from, four areas you drag into, and the options underneath. The panel holds **no state of its own** — every control rebuilds the whole definition and sends one `session_set_pivot`, so it cannot drift from the workbook and each change is one undo step covering the layout *and* the figures. Drag-and-drop between areas (order is nesting order, so a drop position is not cosmetic), double-click to place a field by type (numbers summarize, text groups — Excel's own rule), a three-state sort cycle, per-field subtotal switches shown only where a subtotal can exist, an inline page-filter checklist, and eleven aggregates. Insert ▸ PivotTable builds one on a new sheet — Excel's default and the only destination that cannot collide. **Typing into a report is refused**, as Excel refuses it: a value that stands until an unrelated refresh wipes it is worse than one never accepted. Alt+F5 / Ctrl+Alt+F5 refresh one or all. Report columns are sized from the plan in OOXML's own character unit and ride in the same undo step, because one Ctrl+Z after a layout change must not hand back a column width. **Also fixed a live trap: `BUILD` was a hand-pinned constant**, so a rebuilt engine kept the same module URL and Chrome served the previous glue from its module map — `no-store` does not evict an already-resolved ES module. It now derives from the mtime the dev server already stamps on this file. Verified in-browser end to end. |
| PIV-04 | `GETPIVOTDATA` | Done | The last function the audit listed as *not possible*: it reads a pivot's cache, and there were no pivots. Its argument 2 is a **reference**, not a value — evaluating it would hand back whatever number sits in that cell, which is the very figure the function exists to find by name — so it is read from the AST as `OFFSET` reads its base. Answered by **re-aggregating the source**, not by locating the cell in the report: finding a cell means reproducing the layout's rules about which labels are written and where a subtotal sits, in a second place that would drift. A field not on any axis is `#REF!`, as Excel does, because answering would report a figure the report does not show; so is a missing item, a missing measure and a cell outside any report. Page filters are honoured, since they are part of what the report says. 5 tests. Function coverage **346 → 347 of 356 (97.5%)** — the exclusion table's only row ever removed, which is the argument for naming exclusions rather than counting them. |
| CHT-01 | Charts: authoring, writing real chart parts, and editing | Done | A chart is now in one of two regimes and `ChartView::part` says which. **Read from a file**: the part is authoritative and written back byte for byte, because it carries hundreds of formatting elements and the model holds a handful. **Made here**: there are no bytes to preserve, so the model *is* the chart and the writer builds `chartN.xml` from it. Editing an imported chart moves it into the second regime, which is why the halves never merge — a chart is described by its retained bytes or by the model, never both. The cost is stated rather than hidden: editing an imported chart drops the formatting the model does not carry, and the panel says so before you touch it. **A worksheet may have only one drawing part**, so a chart made on a sheet that already has one is *spliced* into the retained drawing's bytes rather than given a second: rebuilding that drawing from the model would silently delete every text box, shape and form control nothing here models. Writer covers bar/column/line/area/pie/doughnut/scatter with the schema's fixed child order, a pie's absent axes (writing them is invalid, not merely redundant) and a scatter's two value axes (a `catAx` would space the points evenly and lose the relationship the chart exists to show). **Found on the way in: `<xdr:to>` is exclusive** — with `colOff` zero the frame's right edge sits on the *left* edge of that column — and the importer read it as inclusive, drawing every chart a row and a column too large. Invisible until the writer started emitting anchors, at which point a chart would have grown on every save. Legend and axis titles are now parsed, written, and drawn. 5 export tests. |
| CHT-02 | Chart panel, and select/move/resize on the canvas | Done | Insert ▸ Chart reads the range the way Excel does — a text first row is headers, a text first column is labels — so the common case is one click and everything the guess got wrong is in the panel. Panel: type, title, both axis titles, legend position, per-series name and reference, add-from-selection, category labels, move-here, delete. On the canvas: click to select (topmost first, since charts overlap), drag to move, eight handles to resize, Delete to remove, Escape to deselect — all through the transaction layer, so undo takes a chart back whole. `window.openCalcChartFrames()` exposes where each chart was painted: a canvas object has no DOM node, so without it an automated check cannot say where a chart *is*, and verifying drag meant guessing coordinates off a screenshot. Verified in-browser end to end, including a save-and-reopen that returns the identical chart with a stable anchor. |
| CHT-03 | Anchored frames: exact placement, sub-cell offsets, and no vanishing on scroll | Done | Three defects in how an anchored frame becomes pixels, reported as "resize is not accurate" and "the whole chart vanishes when its first row scrolls off". **(1) The far edge was the *leading* edge of the last row and column** — `colXAt(c1)` rather than that column's trailing edge — so every frame drew a row and a column short of what the model held. Harmless while the importer *also* read `<xdr:to>` as inclusive (the two errors cancelled); the moment CHT-01 fixed the importer they stopped cancelling, and a dropped edge redrew somewhere else. **(2) No sub-cell offsets.** OOXML anchors a frame to a cell *plus* an offset in EMUs, and we wrote zeroes: a frame could only start and end on a gridline, so dragging an edge did nothing until it crossed one and then jumped a whole column. `Emu` now travels through model, import, export, payload and the drag; a 17px drag moves the edge 17px. **(3) The frame was abandoned when its top-left was off-screen** — `colXAt` returns `undefined` there and the loop did `continue`, so a chart disappeared entirely the moment its first row scrolled past the top. `fscreenX`/`fscreenXEnd` already extrapolate off-window lines and are now used for both charts and images. Also fixed: the clamped-coordinate/unclamped-offset mismatch in the drag, which put the anchor cell and its offset out of step by the overshoot. 2 export tests; verified in-browser (17px → 17px, chart survives scrolling past the top edge). |
| UX-B02 | Overflowing text vanishes when its own cell scrolls out of the window | Done | Same shape as CHT-03's third defect, reported alongside it. The host draws only the cells it asks the engine for, and it asks for the **visible window** — so a long label in column B spilling across C..N disappeared entirely the moment B scrolled off the left, taking the seven visible columns of text with it. Excel keeps drawing it. Text spills only across *empty* neighbours, so at most one cell per row on each side can be showing text inside the window: the nearest populated one, with everything between it and the window empty by definition. `session_spill_owners` returns that **span** rather than a list of addresses, which is what keeps the cost at two extra `session_cells` calls per geometry rebuild regardless of how many rows are visible. Both sides, because a right-aligned cell spills *leftwards* and reaches back in from beyond the right edge. The draw loop no longer requires an item's own column to be drawn, and the two spill scans use `fscreen*` instead of bailing at the first undrawn column — which is what they had to do while positions came from `colXAt`. Verified in-browser in both directions. |
| CHT-04 | Review pass on CHT-01..03: a deleted imported chart left a dangling anchor | Done | Found reviewing the session's own changes rather than by a failing test. Deleting or editing an imported chart drops its part and the relationship reaching it, but the **anchor** naming that relationship lives inside the retained drawing's bytes, which are deliberately not rewritten. Left behind it points at a relationship that does not exist — and Excel reports that as a file needing repair, not as a chart it cannot draw, which is the exact outcome the rest of this work is built to avoid. The writer now strips any anchor whose `r:id` does not resolve against the drawing's final relationship set, and only those: an anchor with no `r:id` is a shape or a text box, which is precisely the content that must survive. Self-correcting, so it also cleans a drawing that arrived with a dangling anchor. Two near misses writing the scanner, both of which silently stripped nothing: `name>` matches the *opening* tag, and `/name>` misses the close because the namespace prefix sits between the slash and the name. Also fixed `drop_chart_part`, which matched relationships by filename suffix — `../charts/mychart1.xml` ends with `chart1.xml`, so deleting one chart could take an unrelated one off the sheet. 1 test. |
| SDK-002 | Host configuration surface (`SessionConfig`) | Done | Everything a host can decide was a constant somewhere inside the pipeline, so a desktop app opening a file its user chose and a service admitting anonymous uploads behaved identically whether or not that suited either. `SessionConfig` now carries **admission limits** (a security bound — and they were only half applied: `SpreadsheetPackage` kept `limits.package` and dropped the XML bounds, so every part after the first silently used the defaults), **calculation mode**, the **environment** (clock + seed, supplied never sampled), and **undo depth** (unbounded is right for a short session and wrong for a long one — each entry holds a whole inverse operation, and a metadata edit's inverse is a snapshot of the sheet's metadata). Calculation mode is read from the file's own `<calcPr calcMode>`: a workbook saved with calculation off is **not** recalculated on open, because its author turned it off for a reason. Manual mode defers the calculation but not the edit, shows `Calculate` in the status bar as Excel does, and settles the arrears when switched back to automatic. The mode is written back, so turning it off and saving does not produce a file that turns itself on again. Tools ▸ Calculation + F9. 7 tests. |
| SDK-003 | SDK embedding and integration design | Done | All eight decisions answered and folded back into the doc; SDK-004 implements it. [docs/55](55-SDK-EMBEDDING-AND-INTEGRATION-DESIGN.md). Researched against Univer, Handsontable, AG Grid, Player.js and the Next.js/Turbopack wasm situation rather than recalled. Covers packaging (three packages; the wasm-into-someone-else's-bundler problem is the hardest part and Turbopack does not resolve `.wasm` from `import.meta.url` at all), shadow DOM vs iframe with a postMessage protocol specified for the latter, command ids so a host can hide *individual* buttons, typed theme tokens on AG Grid's convention, `before*`/`after*` events with veto and a `source`, read-only as an engine-level refusal rather than a hidden toolbar, and React 19's changed custom-element story. **Eight decisions listed at the end; nothing gets built until they are answered.** A working prototype exists behind it — `<opencalc-sheet>` in a page that sets Comic Sans, RTL, uppercase and `--bg: hotpink`, none of which reaches in — and two defects it surfaced are recorded as evidence for the transport choice: `contain` makes the host a containing block for `position: fixed`, so every dropdown opened as far down the page as the element sat; and `100vh` measures the window even inside a shadow root. |
| SDK-004 | Embeddable SDK: element, theming, chrome, commands, events, access levels | Done | Implementing [docs/55](55-SDK-EMBEDDING-AND-INTEGRATION-DESIGN.md) rather than around it. `<opencalc-sheet>` mounts the *same* editor in a shadow root — the one thing that actually stops a host page's CSS, with `all: initial` for the inherited properties a shadow boundary does not block. Typed theme tokens on AG Grid's convention, **per scheme** because inline custom properties beat the stylesheet's dark block and a host setting a background got it in dark mode too. One shared `CSSStyleSheet` across instances. 189 stable command ids derived from the label path, so a host hides individual buttons. `before*`/`after*` events, cancellable, with a `source` — one per operation carrying a range, not per cell. Three access levels: `edit`, `view` (an access level — the application minus the writing commands) and `preview` (a presentation — no chrome), refused **in the engine** so the UI communicates the mode rather than enforcing it. Four runnable examples in `sdk/`, an integration guide at `webapp/docs.html`. **The examples earned their place immediately**: multiple elements on one page did not work at all (module-scope state, three mounts raced), the wasm URL resolved against the *page* rather than the module (so it 404'd from any directory but `webapp/`), and read-only emptied the menu bar because top-level buttons carry ids like `file`, which is a heading and not a command. |
| SDK-005 | Localization: host-supplied catalogues and a footer picker | Done | Two injection points because they serve two different people: `configure({locale, messages})` for a product that already knows its user's language, and an optional footer picker for one that does not — **off by default**, since a second language control that disagrees with the host's account setting is worse than none. Keyed by **command id**, which is derived from the *English* label path: translating therefore never renumbers the command API, so a German host's `commands({hidden: ["format.bold"]})` keeps matching. A missing key falls back to English, so a partial catalogue degrades to "some of it is translated" rather than to visible keys. Alt mnemonics are recomputed per language — unique letters within each menu, so Datei is `Alt+D`. **Stated gap:** menus, submenus and toolbar tooltips only; panels, dialogs and status messages remain English. Function *names* are deliberately not localized — a file storing `SUMME` is unreadable everywhere else, and someone typing it and getting `#NAME?` concludes the product is broken. |
| SDK-006 | Integration guide, examples, and making them findable | Done | The guide existed and was reachable only through one nav link labelled "SDK" — which the responsive rules hid entirely below 960px, so on a phone there was no route to it at all. The landing page now carries an **Embed** section with three numbered steps, a worked snippet and links to the guide, the live demo and `sdk/`; the footer gained an SDK column; the guide gained a five-step integration walkthrough, a complete copy-paste page, a package-selection table, **CSP requirements** and a troubleshooting table. Writing the CSP section turned up something to fix rather than document: the hoisted `@font-face` block is the SDK's only injected `<style>`, so the element now accepts a `nonce` and a host under a strict policy keeps its bundled fonts. What a nonce cannot cover is the style *attribute* the theme tokens are written to — stated plainly, with setting the custom properties from the host's own stylesheet as the way round it. |
| DOC-023 | Documentation accuracy sweep | Done | Reported as "not all docs are up to date, even readme". The root contract files still opened with **"Documentation phase — no engine code exists yet"** (AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, docs/00, SECURITY.md) across fifteen crates, a shipping editor and an SDK — actively misleading, since AGENTS.md told anyone reading it that authoring docs was the only valid work. Also corrected: the docs index was missing **eleven documents** (45–55, every tracker and the SDK design); the support matrix (since deleted) declared "everything is a target — no code exists" and marked shipped, gated features as unstarted; the CHANGELOG stopped two days and roughly 130 commits short of the present. Two claims in the README were not stale but simply **false**: `casual-calc-ods` was described as doing OpenDocument admission and import when it is a four-line skeleton, and `casual-calc-io` as the format-neutral adapter layer for XLSX/ODS/JSON when it handles delimited text only — the marketing site repeated the first in its pipeline diagram. Both now say what the code does. The npm packages the SDK guide instructs readers to install do not exist yet; rather than delete the guide's shape, the alpha status is stated where someone would otherwise hit it. |
